@

assessment of new products and services, and work is ongoing to improve the full risk
assessment process. There have been delays starting the Payzone risk assessment due to
resourcing and funding challenges, but these were resolved in Q3, and recruitment of a
fixed term contract Financial Crime Manager to complete the work is underway, with a
target for delivery of the assessment by the end of the financial year.

105. A SAR received from one branch identified a complex trading structure with links to
overseas money transmission companies via the Bill Payment Services, exposing an issue
relating to product understanding of exactly what types of businesses are serviced under
re-seller contracts. The controls relating to these contracts will be reviewed as part of the
Payzone and Bill Payments reviews in 2021.

106. The accredited FIO within Security Operations who assisted with the review of SAR
disclosures relating to possible Post Office employees and postmasters has left the business
and the replacement resource will not have FIO accreditation. Financial Crime Compliance
will have to absorb this additional workload moving forward.

107. The POL PCI DSS Compliance project will impact Financial Crime Compliance adding
additional time to some activity, as card data will need to be accessed, processed and stored
in a ‘clean room’ environment. Additionally, during the rollout period, both the existing and
new systems will need to be accessed when undertaking investigation work. We are
working closely with the project team and have called out that additional PO resource may
be needed to support this transition period.

108. As in 2019, there have continued to be significant challenges with the Fit & Proper project
with considerable oversight and support required. The new system for generating annual
declarations, capturing data and generating the required reporting for Post Office internal
governance and HMRC agent data provision was delivered in July 2020, but there are on-
going teething issues (resulting on one occasion in requesting from HMRC approval of a
late submission of monthly data), and the F&P team has moved under a new team leader
as a result of OE, therefore the knowledge and expertise built up by the original team leader
during the last 6 months will be lost. Further oversight and work will be required to ensure
compliance in 2021.

109. Despite improvements relating to direct employee F&P processes implemented in the first
half of 2020, the most recent OE changes have made it difficult to keep this up to date as
individuals leave the business and role titles/responsibilities and line management changes
have occurred. Financial Crime Compliance is working closely with HR to resolve current
discrepancies and implement more robust controls.

110. Further work has been undertaken to improve and implement the Bureau de Change
monitoring reports. This has led to an increase in cases despite the reduction in overall
transaction volumes caused by Covid. The new reports have enabled the identification of
potential vulnerable customers and highlighted customers travelling to multiple branches
to purchase currency, further evidencing the improvements in monitoring controls since the
HMRC audit in 2016. The monthly meetings now held with the Travel Money team ensure
they have direct oversight of the activities to support their produce, and also ensure
Financial Crime Compliance are kept up to date with any product changes or developments.

POST OFFICE LIMITED
RISK & COMPLIANCE COMMITTEE REPORT
Title: Success Factors Update Meeting Date: I 12 January 2021
, Helen Rhodes, People Shared si P 7
Author: Services Centre Director Sponsor: Lisa Cherry, Chief People Officer

Input Sought: Noting

The Risk & Compliance Committee (RCC) is asked to note the progress of the process
improvement work ongoing in People Shared Service Centre (PSSC) for onward submission to
the Audit, Risk & Compliance Committee (ARC).

Previous Governance Oversight

« The SuccessFactors (SF) Response Paper was presented in the September 2020 ARC
meeting. It was agreed that SF should be utilised to its full potential and gave the go
ahead for process/ system enhancement work in PSSC.

e The purpose of the process and system enhancement work in PSSC is to ensure best use
from SF and to ensure that processes become more efficient whilst maximising the use
of automation, mitigating risks and assuring compliance.

Executive Summary

The Process Improvement project within PSSC began in September 2020. The project identified
202 processes and sub-processes within the service which all needed to be documented,
reviewed and, where possible, improved.

By 31% December 2020, 74 processes have been fully reviewed and documented. 38
improvements (including ‘work in progress’ tickets) have been made to SF configuration
through the small changes process with current suppliers, leading to no additional spend for
improvements. The introduction of Process Improvement specialists and Service Centre
expertise has increased rigour and control to the development of efficient processes and BAU
operations of SF, leading to a culture of continuous improvement,

Through the Organisational Effectiveness programme and as a result of process efficiencies, it
has been identified that PSSC is able to reduce by 6 roles in Tranche 2.

Further work has also taken place to address the recommendations in the Deloitte Report. As
a result, it has been identified that along with the process improvement work, Service Now HR
module could be introduced as a complementary and integrated technology to SF. This will be
a key technical enabler for the PSSC, providing consistency of approach to customers, full audit
trails and the ability to report key service measures. This piece of work is currently being
scoped, as the SNOW has been confirmed as fit for purpose for our needs. A business case is
being developed in order to articulate the full cost of the change to ensure benefits delivered
(e.g. change resources, related process change and tool roll out). Target submission date is
the end of January 2021. This will be managed and reported on via the OE Portfolio and in
alignment with TOM activities.

Questions addressed

a

What progress on process improvement has been made since the last paper?

2. What are the resulting technological enablers that need scoping and investigating as a
result of the process improvement and in order to minimise risk?

3. How to make the PSSC more efficient, maintain service levels, maximise compliance and
mitigate any risks?

4. What technology changes need to be made to address the recommendations of the Deloitte
report and the needs for process improvement?

5. What does the future Target Operating Model look like for the PSSC?

Report
What progress on process improvement has been made since the last paper?

1. Ultimately, we are aiming to review 100% of the processes within the PSSC to minimise
organisational risk and ensure that all employees continue to receive the right service at
the right time in the most efficient way possible.

2. 96 Priority 1 and 2 processes for the process improvement review have been identified
within PSSC and subsequent work streams commenced to analyse and improve these
processes. To date, 74 processes have been reviewed - 13 Defined Benefit Pension Scheme
processes and 61 additional processes. Priority 1 and 2 processes which have been
completed or are in flight include work streams with PSSC Projects, Payroll, People
Changes, Systems and MI. Improvements include 38 SuccessFactors configuration changes
and changes to ways of working to reduce waste, remove risk and bridge process gaps.
The advantages of using this method of change to SF means that these are all technically
classed as minor changes which reduces the risk of any update issues in the future as they
can easily be tested when mass updates are made and the configuration is easily
manipulated to deal with changes meaning there is less incremental costs in the future.

3. The remaining processes will be subject to review throughout Q4 2020/1 with an objective
to be completed at the end of Q1 2021/2 (see Appendix for high level plan). PSSC is
working closely with Data Protection, Finance, Risk and Internal Audit to ensure that the
necessary TrAction Controls are in place for each process as part of the review. Internal
controls for all PSSC Managers have also been developed to ensure appropriate checkpoints
throughout the processes.

4. Some role-based permission (RBPs) changes have been completed to facilitate the move
towards one ‘Tier 1’ PSSC Team processing joiners, movers, leavers (JML), changes and
queries. Process improvement reviews on work streams that support this model are in
progress. This will drive improved efficiency through economies of scale and removal of
single points of failure across basic administration processes. The ability to automate a
number of these processes will reduce the risk of manual error, increase collaboration
across teams through automation (e.g. IT) and improve the employee experience through
more automated process and point in time interventions.

What are the resulting technological enablers that need scoping and investigating as
a result of the process improvement and in order to minimise risk?

5. Removal of the current risks associated with single points of failure within PSSC will be
overcome by the ongoing enabling activity related to role-based permissions, and cross
team training on SF activity relating to process work streams. This is being achieved by an
iterative approach to the introduction of the revised processes and when the configuration
changes to SF are fully tested and enabled.

6. Embedding the new processes and technology is a key driver to ensuring that risks are
reduced quickly but due to the cyclical nature of some of the processes this is forecast to
take some time and with the potential implementation of new technology through
ServiceNow, it is forecast that risks will not be fully minimised until the end of Q3 2021/2.
However, the current plan ensures that the more prevalent risks are being minimised first.

7. SF enabling works to reduce instances of data being calculated and entered manually,
multiple times will reduce the risk of human error which can result in incorrect processing
(e.g. incorrect entries on SF and in documentation). These changes have all been
undertaken as part of process improvement through our small changes process with our
systems integration partner. Each change has gone through full testing by both our partner
and in-house team and been subject to post implementation review to ensure that changes
are working as planned and have no unforeseen consequences. This enabling activity will
continue throughout the process improvement work with the objective to be completed by
the end of Q1 2021/2.

How to make the PSSC more efficient, maintain service levels, maximise compliance
and mitigate any risks?

8. We will continue the process improvement project during Q4 20/21 and into 21/22 to
complete the review and (where appropriate) improvement of PSSC processes. Align PSSC
target operating model to work with the wider People TOM (in progress) and the reviewed
PSSC processes, ensuring the PSSC has the right people, in the right structure to support
the People Team and the wider business. This will require a skills gap analysis within the
team alongside T2 activity.

9. During Q4 2020/1, the remaining P2 process work streams have been prioritised in line
with those that represent greatest risk for POL (predominantly payroll processes) and P3
work streams with greatest opportunity (based on effort/impact assessment).

10. Additional SF configuration, identified by Deloitte review and ongoing process improvement
project that cannot be completed as part of existing support contract (due to time, cost,
and complexity) is being scoped currently in order to secure funding in Q1/2 2021/2.

11. From the beginning of Q4 2020/1, we will be executing the plan to upskill and develop
knowledge within PSSC Systems Team, to reduce reliance on third party support for SF
enhancements (currently c.90% of SF change tickets are completed by third party partner).
Target is for c.80% of change/enhancement tickets (not defects) to be completed in house
by the end of 2021/2. All configuration changes which will be carried out in-house will be
carried out in conjunction with SF best practice and with the same assurances and risk
mitigations applied to ensure that future updates will not be impacted without appropriate

What technology changes need to be made to address the recommendations of the
Deloitte report and the needs for process improvement?

12. Further work has also taken place to address the recommendations in the Deloitte Report.
As a result, it has been identified that along with the process improvement work, Service
Now HR module could be introduced as a complementary and integrated technology to SF.
Implementation of ServiceNow as a service management tool could be used for reactive
work across PSSC. This will overcome the current lack of transparency (for customers) and
control (for PSSC Management Team) regarding ticket progress and resolution. The risk of
tickets being missed, unsatisfactorily completed, or missing SLAs, which will be
implemented as part of the project, could be minimised and stakeholders could have a clear
overview of open activity, as well as a full audit trail of completed activity. This piece of
work is currently being scoped and a business case being developed with a target
submission date of the end of January 2021. Currently the risks identified through lack of
transparency and control of tickets is being managed manually through checks and
balances. This process although effective, is inefficient and can lead to human error.

13. The potential introduction of ServiceNow will mean that some processes will need to be
revisited to ensure that maximum efficiency can be gained by using the technology
available. Further process improvement is likely therefore to continue in Q2 2021/2 before
regular reviews and continuous improvement becomes part of business as usual.

14. A business case is being developed in order to articulate the full cost of the change to
ensure benefits delivered (e.g. change resources, related process change and tool roll out).
Target submission date is the end of January 2021. This will be managed and reported on
via the OE Portfolio and in alignment with TOM activities.

15. The introduction of the ServiceNow HR module and the end-to-end process reviews could
enable a lot of the recommendations in the Deloitte Report to be implemented without the
incremental spend in SF. However, some investment in configuration of SF which sit outside
of the current small changes portfolio will enable the following:

e People/compensation data: Allow additional pay data to be stored/calculated/pulled
from tables, reducing risk due to manual calculation/data entry;

« RBPs: Further work on RBPs, including the definition and creation of additional RBPs
where necessary, to remove risk caused by single points of failure within PSSC;

« New/enhanced workflows and notifications: Ensure right stakeholders are
involved/informed throughout processes by leveraging SF workflow capability;

e Optimise mass org change/restructure process: SF enhancements to ensure mass
upload functionality works for POL and offline, upstream processes (i.e. OD work)
enable this approach

16. This potential new technology will also ensure that c.50% of the Deloitte recommendations
will have been implemented and if successful in gaining funding will be completed and
embedded by the end of Q3 2021/2.

17. Should the investment in ServiceNow not be available processes will be reviewed to ensure
maximum benefits can be gained without the use of automation. This will not be as efficient
as it could be with the automation and will mean some risks will not be fully mitigated but
further review will need to take place to ensure mitigation benefits are maximised.

What does the future Target Operating Model look like for the PSSC?

18. Through the Organisational Effectiveness programme and as a result of process efficiencies,
it has been identified that PSSC is able to reduce by 6 roles in Tranche 2. The business case
for this will be coming to Board into January. These 6 roles have been identified through
the analysis of capacity and the efficiencies already gained by the process improvements
that have been embedded.

19. The wider People TOM work stream, in which the PSSC role reduction sits, continues to
progress in line with the timelines set out for the whole Organisational Design project. The
People work streams follows in Phase Three (rightsizing support functions) after Phase One
(identifying what products we sell) and Phase Two (identifying how we sell them) are
embedded. We therefore anticipate that the People work stream will dovetail into Tranche
3 - date TBD but before January 2022.

20. For this reason, we are proposing to be very clear in our Tranche 2 narrative for change in
PSSC that we are proposing the headcount reduction of 6 roles is due to being over-
resourced for current capacity only. We will ensure that the function is aware that more
change is inevitable as we are still on the transformation journey. We hope to be able to
manage this in a low risk method, anticipating that there may be some leavers who want
to take up Voluntary Redundancy at this time.

21. Returning to the role of the PSSC in the wider People TOM, the commitment for the PSSC
is to become the “engine room” of the People function, ensuring that all requests for HR
support come through the engine model and are escalated according to a tiered system.

22. The tiered model of support becomes an embedded principle so that:
e Tier 0 - Will be self-service through ServiceNow and the PeopleHub
« Tier 1 — Will be transactional processing through a dedicated team in the PSSC e.g.
workflow requests or contract extensions
e Tier 2 - Will be more complex processing requiring technical knowledge and
experience - e.g. payroll or pensions

23. The final core principle is the use of technology in order to continue to minimise risk and to
maximise efficiency at all times.

Title: es Update and Annual Tax Meeting Date: I 12 January 2021
trategy
Brian Kelly, Director, Treasury, Tax " 2 . .
Author: & Working Capital Sponsor: ed Carneren, Cite Finances
Andy Jamieson, Head of Tax

Input Sought: Noting

The Risk & Compliance Committee (RCC) is asked to note the Tax Update and approve the
annual review of the Tax Strategy for onward submission to the Audit, Risk & Compliance
Committee (ARC).

Executive Summary

This paper is issued as a follow up to the paper presented to the RCC in January 2020. It
provides an update on tax, as well as on the annual review of the Post Office Limited (POL) Tax
Strategy last published in January 2020 (the “Tax Strategy”).

Questions addressed

1. What are the strategic tax challenges for POL?

2. What are the current key tax issues for POL and what are we doing to address these?
3. What changes have been made to the tax team since the last update?
4.
5

. What other tax updates should the RCC/ ARC be aware of?
What is the requirement for reviewing and updating the published tax strategy?

Report
What are the strategic tax challenges for POL?

Improving controls around tax compliance

1. In the last few annual updates we reported that the primary tax risk was centred around
VAT, however we have made good progress particularly enabled with CFS now being the
single source of truth - it has allowed us to tighten controls and improve processes, reducing
the risk of errors. In 2018 it took 5 to 7 days each month to pull together the VAT return
information which would then be aggregated into the quarterly return. In 2020 it takes the
same length of time to compile the whole quarterly return. The introduction of Web3 has
also enabled us to bring in some automation in tax coding of purchases, further reducing
risk, and providing more control for the tax team. We recently walked HMRC through Web3
and they were impressed with the controls and reduced risk.

2. It has become clear that we did not have sufficient expertise and knowledge in the business
covering employment taxes. HMRC have expressed concern in the past over the lack of
expert resource in this tax which has been evidenced by the disclosure earlier in 2020 and

by the number of projects which require specialist input as well as legislative changes
introduced to IR35 (Off Payroll Workers). We have therefore recruited an employment taxes

Confidential

83 Post Office Limited - Risk and Compliance Committee-12/01/21

POL-BSFF-0238512_0129
Tab 9 Te

POL00423694
POL00423694

odate & Tax Strategy

@

specialist to work in the tax team and to support HR colleagues, who should be in post by
5 March 2021.

3. HMRC introduced the Business Risk Review + regime in 2019, with the first reports in 2020.
The revised regime has provided greater detail as to HMRC’s requirements regarding the
evidence and behaviours they wish to see in order to categorise the risk rating of the
business. The risk rating determines how much audit focus HMRC places on the business
and is used internally by HMRC to plan its interactions with businesses.

4. Risk is rated in three areas; Systems; Governance; and Approach to Tax Compliance. We
were rated as low risk in approach to tax compliance, reflecting the work we have put in to
reduce risk and tighten controls over the last year, however, we were rated as moderate-
high risk for System and Governance due to particular concerns arising from the Horizon
issues highlighted in the GLO (based on the public statements issued), the introduction of
a new system, Web3, and error reporting in employment taxes. As more than one rating
was in the moderate-high category this was also given as our overall rating. We have
already provided reassurances over the integrity of Horizon and Web3 has brought reduced
risk and we are recruiting an employment taxes specialist, all of which have helped to
address HMRC’s concerns. We therefore expect to achieve a lower rating in the 20/21
review to be reported on in April 2021.

Improving tax forecasting and maximising use of available reliefs

5. We have worked with Financial Planning & Analysis colleagues this year to build more
accurate forecasting models for VAT and for corporation tax (CT). Historically our VAT
position has remained very stable with little fluctuation on a year on year basis. However,
the impact of Covid19 combined with the growth of Banking Framework income and the
potential disposal of Telco have introduced variables which could significantly impact our
VAT recovery rate, possibly reducing it by around 5%, with every 1% reduction giving us
c.£500k additional VAT costs to bear. We have informed management of the possible
impacts, based on our improved forecasting models and this on a monthly basis.

6. As the business has continues to improve its book profitability there is an increasing focus
on management of corporation tax (CT). We continue to make tax losses, despite book
profits, but we expect to be in a CT paying position in 22/23. We are therefore working to
ensure we are able to make the best use of our historic tax losses (c.£1bn) which we have
accumulated.

What are the current key tax issues for POL and what have we done to address these?

VAT

7. The primary VAT risk is to ensure that POL pays and claims the correct amount, this year

we have focussed on;

* ensuring our systems for reporting VAT to HMRC are compliant with Phase 2 of HMRC’s
Making Tax Digital requirements;

¢ building system auto posting of tax codes into Web3 (the new purchasing system) and
training staff;

e and providing advice to the business regarding the revised Mails Distribution Agreement
which contains several clauses relating to VAT charges between the two parties.

Systems/ MTD

Confidential

Post Office Limited - Risk and Compliance Committee-12/01/2 131 of 183

POL-BSFF-0238512_0130
POL00423694
POL00423694

Tab 9 Te

odate & Tax Strategy

@

8. We have used, and further refined, BI tools in VAT reporting to reduce manual intervention
as per HMRC’s requirements. This has in turn reduced the time in producing the VAT return
and enabled us to focus more on checking and educating, rather than simply compiling the
returns.

Web3 procurement system

9. We have also used the introduction of Web3 to remove the need for requisitioners to choose
a tax code for buying an item or service, by auto populating tax codes against cost centres.
We control and maintain the master list of tax codes. Not all cost centres can use the single
rules, so there are occasions where manual over-writing is required, but this is the
exception and not the rule. HMRC are currently undertaking an audit of the system, see
para 26 below.

Mails Distribution Agreement 2 (MDA 2)

10. The original MDA contained numerous VAT clauses including a calculation clause which
resulted in an annual payment, based on VAT transactions between the parties, c.£2.5m to
Royal Mail Group (RMG). In MDA 2 we have replaced a substantial amount of the VAT
clauses, removing the VAT calculation clause with a commercial settlement. This has
reduced POL’s financial risk as the general shift from letters to parcels increased the amount
payable under the clause.

11. Both parties have worked to ensure that MDA 2 does not affect the VAT benefit arising from
the special treatment of the income POL receives from RMG, referred to as the “Stamp
Solution”. As reported in the last update this provides an annual saving of c.£22m.

Post bill payment VAT treatment

12. In the January 2020 update we made the RCC/ARC aware of certain potential changes to
the VAT treatment of commissions on bill payments. In June 2020 HMRC wrote to confirm
VAT applies to this income, but allowing us to implement the change in our own time.

13. It is a complex project to amend the VAT treatment across, POL, Payzone and on our
commission payments to Agents. We are on target for implementing the change with effect
from mid-February 2021. The VAT change in income will impact our partial exemption VAT
recovery rate, providing a c.£1m/ annum reduction in our overall VAT bill. There are very
few clients which will be impacted by the change, i.e. most will be able to claim any VAT
charged to them, in full, from HMRC.

14. We wrote to HMRC stating our intention to submit a historic VAT claim in relation to this
issue. The claim would be based on the fact that, as there has been no change to our
business operations, HMRC ruled on the VAT treatment of such supplies, therefore this
treatment should always have applied. If we are successful, then reworking our partial
exemption VAT recovery may provide a windfall cash repayment c.£3.5m. HMRC’s initial
response was that the claim would be valid, but we would have re-issue VAT invoices, and
seek payment, covering the 4 year period of the claim to all clients. We are considering
the practicality of this requirement and will decide in January after consulting with
commercial and finance colleagues, whether to proceed.

15. We have identified only one VAT error this year which was a misclassification of income

Key Employment Taxes issues

Employees considered to have more than one permanent place of work

16. In 2019 we identified an issue whereby certain employees, who had been appointed on
home-based contracts, were required to work regularly at office locations. Where there is
a regular permanent second place of work and the individual claims expenses for travel,
accommodation and subsistence, it is a taxable benefit-in-kind. HMRC deem the employee
to have more than one permanent place of work. A payment in respect of tax was made
for 19/20 as part of the normal annual settlement process and we disclosed an error and
made a payment to HMRC of £403k for 15/16, 16/17 to 17/18.

17. Due to the impact of Covid19 there has been little travel in 20/21. We have provided
guidance to HR colleagues regarding individuals who may require their contracts amending
to reflect dual places of work and prevent additional tax being payable by the business, but
at present due to the uncertainty changes have not been made. We continue to liaise and
provide guidance to our HR colleagues on this issue.

IR35 - employment status of contractors

18. In 2017 HMRC updated IR35 legislation to assess whether roles filled by a contractor should
be on the payroll or were a true 3" party relationship. We are required to carry out
assessments, using HMRC’s online tool, when engaging contractors to assess their
employment status. HMRC, although delaying the introduction of the new rules to the
private sector, introduced a revised online assessment tool which must now be used to
determine the employment status of contractors.

19. HMRC is currently carrying out a review of our application of the rules assessing a sample
of contractors. There have been significant delays in the review due to Covid19. HMRC
have asked for substantial additional information which we are collating to respond. We
are comfortable that we applied the HMRC tests in a consistent manner in line with the
guidance. Colleagues in the SPO are preparing the response to HMRC, to be sent in January.

Historic Settlement Scheme

20. Payments to claimants under the scheme may, in certain circumstances, be liable to tax
and NI withholding responsibilities for us. We have been working with Deloitte and Herbert
Smith Freehills to determine and agree the correct tax treatment and reporting mechanisms
with HMRC. There are several different claim types which could be made in relation to
different heads of loss. In order to cover the possible different tax treatments arising have
built a matrix for the project team to ensure the correct treatment is applied to all claims.

Corporation Tax Losses

21. As at the 2018/19 tax year POL had accumulated tax losses of around £900 million. We
estimate that, once the 2019/20 tax computation has been finalised and submitted, POL
will have losses carried forward of in excess of £1 billion.

22. From April 2017 the utilisation of historic tax losses carried forward was restricted to a relief
of £5 million against the initial profit and then a 50% restriction of any profits above £5
million. This means that POL will pay corporation tax sooner than it would have done under
the old loss rules.

23. Despite being increasingly profitable at an EBITDAS level, POL continues to create tax losses
whilst it incurs significant exceptional and transformational spend which is deductible for
tax purposes. We have a built a model which is updated each period using the known
funding amounts and income forecasts prepared by Financial Planning & Analysis
colleagues. We do not anticipate paying corporation tax until 2022/23 at the earliest.
Based on current income predictions the tax payable will be less than £3.5m.

What changes have been made to the tax team since the last update?

24. HMRC have previously indicated that, based on the size and complexity of the business,
POL's tax team was under-resourced. We have now recruited a full-time Employment tax
specialist team member to support the Head of Tax in this high risk area who is due to start
at POL in early March 2021.

Tax advisors. We continue to take specialist advice where appropriate, based on the risk
and complexity of the issues under consideration.

What other tax related issues should the RCC/ARC be made aware of?

Governance and Tax controls

26. The follow up to HMRC’s 2016 Tax Governance report is still in draft, based on recent
discussions we do not expect them to issue it until at least March 2021. The 2016 report
highlighted a lack of documented process around tax and tax risk reporting. In the
intervening period we have addressed each issue HMRC raised and informed HMRC of the
improvements/ changes to processes we have made. We produce a quarterly tax summary
report highlighting activity across all taxes which is circulated to the CFO, Head of Legal
Services and the Group Chief People Officer. We also hold regular meetings with Risk
colleagues and sign off tax controls, every month through PwC's ‘TrAction’ tool with
supporting evidence uploaded into the system.

HMRC VAT Audit

27. New audits planned for 2020 were placed on hold due to Covid19. We have now agreed a
program of more limited scope on-line audits which commenced in December with a
walkthrough of Web3 and its interface with CFS using Microsoft Teams. HMRC reported they
were impressed with the improvements we have made.

Making Tax Digital

28. Making Tax Digital (MTD) is a key part of the government's strategy to make it easier for
individuals and businesses to get their tax right and keep on top of their affairs. HMRC’s
stated ambition is to become one of the most ‘digitally advanced tax administrations in the
world, modernising the tax system to make it more effective, efficient and to ease
compliance’.

Brexit

31. Due to the Government's requirements for electronic submissions covering the movements
of stock from GB to NI, announced in November 2020, there are new reporting
requirements being placed on the business. In addition, we will have to add VAT to the
value of the goods moved and to claim back the some of this VAT in our VAT return and
some in a later annual adjustment. These requirements are quite onerous and require new
processes to be developed to in order to comply. We have contacted HMRC to see if there
are any concessions available as there is no additional tax or Customs Duty payable by POL
and have been told possible revisions to the new rules are being reviewed. We are
concerned that the new system and reporting requirements will place undue burdens on
the Tax team and may bring us into conflict with HMRC’s Making Tax Digital requirements.

ARC in November 2017. The Tax team has reviewed the current Strategy and have no
proposed amendments as it remains fit for purpose and reflects our position and after sign-
off will be published on the Corporate website and highlighted in a One News article.

Appendix 1 - Post Office Group Tax Strategy

This publication sets out the tax strategy of Post Office Limited and its UK subsidiary
undertakings (referred to hereafter as the “Group” or “Post Office”) for the financial year
2021/22, and in making this strategy available the UK Group is fulfilling its responsibilities
under the Finance Act 2016, Chapter 24, Schedule 19, Part 2, Paragraphs 16 & 17.

This tax strategy applies to UK taxes applicable to the Post Office and its affiliated entities both
in the UK and overseas. The document is ultimately owned by the Board of Directors of Post
Office Limited (“the Board”).

The tax strategy is reviewed annually, updated as appropriate and approved by the Board each
January to cover the next financial year. The Board, along with assistance from the Group
Finance teams, take ultimate responsibility for setting, monitoring and amending the strategy
as required.

In summary, the Post Office is committed to:

« following all applicable laws and regulations relating to its tax activities;

* continuing to have an open and honest relationship with HM Revenue & Customs driven
by collaboration and integrity; and

* applying diligence and care in our management of taxes, and ensuring that our tax
governance is appropriate.

How the Post Office manages its tax risks

The Group’s on-going approach to UK tax risk management and governance is based on the
principles of reasonable care and materiality. The Post Office maintains on-going application of
tax governance, including frequent risk metric assessments and the review of applications of
strong internal control procedures, in order to substantially reduce tax risk to materially
acceptable levels.

As part of this governance, the Post Office has identified tax risks, which are maintained
internally on risk registers, with their materiality being assessed based on a corporate risk
matrix. The matrix then records the potential impact, subject to two contributory factors, the
exposure if the tax risk crystallises and the relative likelihood of the risk crystallising.

Monthly review processes are carried out, based on the risk areas, and confirmation reports
and evidence are logged in the external auditor's software. A quarterly tax issues report is then
presented with significant / material issues to the Chief Financial Officer for his consideration,
further discussion at Board level and with HM Revenue & Customs should the issue merit
engagement of the tax authorities. Where decisions are deemed to be complex or have an
element of uncertainty assistance from third parties may be sought to aid the Post Office’s
decision-making process.

Tax planning

Given that the Post Office is owned by the British Government's Department for Business,
Energy & Industrial Strategy, it understands the importance of its transparent business
operations.

The Post Office will not engage in artificial transactions the sole purpose of which is to reduce
UK tax. As well as the above the Post Office will not engage in tax efficiencies if the underlying
commercial objectives do not support the Group’s position, or if the arrangements impact upon
the Post Office’s reputation, brand, corporate and social responsibilities, or future working
relationships with HM Revenue & Customs.

Approach towards dealings with HMRC

The Post Office have always been and remain committed to maintaining integrity and
transparency when dealing with HMRC. The Post Office underlines these principles by agreeing
to:

« Accurately disclose all information required in correspondence and returns, and
efficiently respond to communications as and when required. Where additional work is
required, such as in the event of a disagreement, we will look to resolve this in the most
professional and efficient way possible.

« Be open and transparent about decision-making, governance and tax planning, firstly by
ensuring that it is liaising directly with our dedicated HMRC team and secondly by
publishing our tax strategy easily accessible within the public domain.

« Ensure all interactions with HMRC are conducted in an open, collaborative and
professional manner.

Executive Summary

The performance improvement reported to the ARC in September 2020 on branch balancing
has continued through to week 38 of this financial year and each trading period of the current
financial year has seen a year on year improvement.

Taken at a branch level, this means that the average branch has reduced its losses from
£135.96 per trading period last financial year to £63.44 per trading period this financial year.
This represents an annualised improvement per branch of £870.24.

The drivers of this performance are:

e Improved support for branches;
« Improved Post Office processes; and
e Lower transaction volumes during the Covid-19 pandemic

The improved branch balancing performance has resulted in lower balances being posted to
Postmaster accounts. However, recoveries continue to be lower than balances posted to
Postmaster accounts and this means that the total balances held on Postmaster accounts
continue to increase.

A new policy has been developed to support the new processes being deployed by the
Postmaster Account Support Team and this is detailed in a separate ARC paper on Postmaster
policies.

To continue to improve performance in relation to branch balancing, Post Office will continue
to find better ways to support branches with their branch accounting and to improve its own
processes where they impact branch accounting. Post Office will also look to use data mining
to identify the propositions and processes that most impact branch balancing.

Tab 10 Update on branch losses and balances on Postmaster accounts

Questions addressed

1. Since the last update to the ARC in September 2020, what trends have been identified in
balances posted to Postmaster accounts?

2. What progress has been made on reducing branch losses and balances posted to
Postmaster accounts?

Report

1. The aim of this paper is to provide a more comprehensive overview of balances posted to
Postmaster accounts and to update the ARC on the methods being deployed to reduce
balances posted to Postmaster accounts.

Branch Accounting Processes

2. Post Office branches complete branch accounting known as branch balancing, a Trading
Period or a Trading Statement on a periodic basis. The branches are split into four balancing
groups, which means that in a four week trading period, different groups of branches
balance in each week and in a five week trading period, the same groups balance in each
of the first four weeks of the trading period. Effectively each branch balances on a five-
weekly, four-weekly, four-weekly basis.

3. The activity involved in completing branch balancing involves physically counting all cash
and stock in a branch and comparing that to the balance of cash and stock calculated by
Horizon. At this point, a branch will either balance (the physical cash and stock matches
the Horizon generated cash and stock), have a gain (the physical cash and stock is greater
than the Horizon generated cash and stock) or have a loss (the physical cash and stock is
less than the Horizon generated cash and stock).

4. For a branch to be able to be ‘rolled’ into the next trading period, the branch needs to
balance. So, where there is a gain or a loss, the branch can choose to:

a. Add or remove cash from the branch (known as settling to cash)

b. Add a cheque to the branch (known as settling to cheque)

c. Post the balance to the Postmaster’s customer account (known as settling
centrally)

d. Post the balance to a nominee account if they are a multiple partner with a
central account (known as settling to nominee)

5. The separate paper on Postmaster policies to this Committee details the new policy for
managing and investigating balances posted to Postmaster’s customer accounts.

Branch Balancing Performance

6. The last update to the ARC on Postmaster accounts in September 2020 reported that the
number of branches experiencing losses when completing trading period accounting was
falling and that the average value of losses experienced by the branches who had losses
were also falling.

7. This pattern of lower branch losses and lower balances being settled to Postmaster accounts
has continued through to week 38 (week commencing 14'" December 2020).

8. The following table shows the balancing results to week 38 this financial year versus the
balancing results to week 38 the previous financial year:

Financial Year to Week 38 Gross losses Gross gains Net balance
FY2020-21 £16,951,639.52 £10,567,349.95 £6,384,289.57
FY2019-20 £25,549,872.16 £11,868,746.34 £13,681,125.82

9. This shows that the value of the gains and losses experienced by branches when balancing
has reduced year on year.

10. For gains, the reduction is driven by a decrease in the average value of the gains with the
average gain reducing to £356.13 from £437.67 (-18.6% YoY) and is partly offset by an
increase in the number of gains from 27,118 to 29,673 (+9.4% YoY).

11. For losses, the reduction is driven by a decrease in both the average value of the losses
from £749.44 to £556.92 (-25.7%) and the number of losses from 34,092 to 30,438 (-
10.7%).

12. When also considering the ~40% of branches who balance exactly in a trading period, this
means that the average branch is posting a loss of £63.44 per trading period this financial
year versus a loss of £135.96 per trading period last financial year. This is an improvement
of 53.3%.

13. The year on year improvement in performance is consistent, with each trading period of
2020-21 seeing an improvement year on year.

Drivers of Branch Balancing Performance
14. This performance has a number of drivers but it is difficult to precisely quantify the impact
of each of the drivers.

15. Firstly, Post Office has purposefully and deliberately increased the level of support it offers
to branches in relation to branch accounting.

16. There has been a focus to improve the level of compliance with trading period accounting
and cash declarations across the network. This focus has seen Post Office make 8,211
compliance calls to branches to the end of Period 8 this financial year against 786
compliance calls in the same time period last year. Branch compliance has improved as a
result with:

a. the number of branches having not done cash declarations for over 10 days
falling from 280 on 7" April 2020 (the first date this measure was established)
to 36 as at Period 9, and

b. the number of branches having not rolled over into the correct trading period
falling from 104 on 7 April 2020 to 69 as at Period 9.

17. This improved level of compliance improves the reliability of the data used by Post Office
to monitor the network and support Postmasters.

18. In December 2019, Post Office introduced a proactive intervention activity known as a
SPEAR visit (SPEAR stands for Support, Prevent, Educate, Advise and Resolve). These visits

Confidential

83 Post Office Limited - Risk and Compliance Committee-12/01/21

POL-BSFF-0238512_0139
POL00423694
POL00423694

@

are pre-arranged and completed by the Audit Support team and are focused on helping
branches who appear to be experiencing difficulties with their branch accounting.

19. There have been 518 SPEAR visits to the end of Period 8 and these have been supplemented
by 311 SPEAR calls as a result of the Covid-19 pandemic. SPEAR activity is able to be
completed with a shorter lead time than audit activity and requires less resource, so the
level of responsiveness when a branch appears to be experiencing difficulties with their
branch accounting is improved.

20. In April 2019, Post Office introduced a Tier 2 Balancing Support team into the Branch
Support Centre. Tier 2 Balancing Support is a reactive support service that is in place to
help Postmasters establish the cause of any gains or losses that they experience when
balancing.

21. There have been 1,834 cases raised with the Tier 2 Balancing Support team to the end of
Period 8. Cases are raised prior to a branch completing their trading period accounting
(usually at weekly balancing or a cash declaration), so £3,172,975.80 of discrepancies that
have been resolved by the Tier 2 Balancing Support team to the end of Period 8 will have
directly contributed to the improved balancing results.

22. Secondly, Post Office has improved processes that support accurate branch accounting.

23. The volume of cash held in the network has reduced significantly year on year with average
branch holdings at £39,167.91 at the end of Period 8 this year versus £43,576.14 at the
end of Period 8 last year (-10.12%). This has been delivered through collaboration between
the Supply Chain teams, the Area Manager team and the Network Monitoring and Support
teams and was particularly key when releasing trapped cash from within the network during
the first Covid-19 lockdown.

24. The reduced levels of cash in the network make cash handling easier for branches and allow
the network monitoring teams to more easily identify branches with cash above the level
expected for the branch.

25. Over the same period of time, branches with ‘excess’ cash of over £50k has fallen from 783
branches to 536 branches (-31.5%), with the value of the cash held at these branches
falling from £84m to £53m (-36.9%).

26. The timeliness of the issuing of transaction corrections from the Branch Reconciliation Team
has also improved. This is vital for supporting branches with reducing variability and
maintaining accuracy in their branch accounting.

27.The number of items older than 2 months in the accounts from which transaction
corrections are issued has fallen from 7,340 at the end of Period 8 last year to 1,447 at the
end of Period 8 this year.

28. The improvement in the timeliness of transaction corrections is in spite of an increase in
the volume of transaction corrections, which is being driven by cash pouch discrepancies.

29. Training has also improved for branches in relation to branch accounting. Elearning modules

Further Improvements

36. The changes made by Post Office over the last 20 months have seen the branch balancing
performance improve significantly. The levers of increased support and improved Post
Office processes will continue to be the key levers in driving further improvements.

37. Post Office will continue to find better ways to improve branch accounting compliance,

term needs replacing to more accurately describe what a branch does when ‘settling
centrally’, which is to move a balance, for investigation, to their Postmaster’s account to
allow them to begin the next trading period with a zeroed trading position. The review will
also look to quickly remove the minimum value that can be settled centrally from £150 to
£0.

40. There is also an opportunity to more deeply understand the root causes of accounting
discrepancies through data mining, moving the detect and respond model currently used
by Post Office to a predict and prevent model. Understanding the root causes will enable
Post Office to understand which propositions and processes cause discrepancies and what
corrective action is required to mitigate the causes.

for onward submission to the Audit, Risk & Compliance Committee (ARC).

Executive Summary

Following the Group Litigation Order (GLO), Post Office set about ensuring that its processes
complied with the findings of the GLO.

The focus on processes has delivered a large number of changes to the support that Post Office
offers postmasters over the last 20 months. However, this work has mainly been done without
updating the policies that sit over the processes. This has often been the case because no policy
existed in the first instance or the policy was so dated that it was irrelevant to the processes
undertaken pre or post the GLO.

Having policies in place for the support Post Office provides postmasters will bring Post Office
in line with best practice franchise businesses. The purposes of the policies are to provide
guidance, set down principles and highlight risk areas, while also ensuring that Post Office is
able to support postmasters effectively and compliantly with the GLO.

As part of an overall review of postmaster support policy requirements, this paper seeks
approval for three new postmaster policies to reflect how Post Office will provide support to
postmasters in relation to their branch accounting. There were previously no policies in this
area.

Questions addressed

1. What policies are required to support the changes made to postmaster support following
the GLO?

2. What policies have recently been developed and now require approval?

3. What further policy work is required to ensure there is a full complement of postmaster
support policies in place and how will these be continually reviewed in the future?

Internal

Report

1. Following the Common Issues Judgment and Horizon Issues Judgment in the GLO, Post

Office focused on improving processes to ensure compliance with the outcomes of the
judgments.

2. Whilst process improvements were delivered, Post Office has identified that there was an
absence of overarching policies for these processes to sit under. Where policies previously
existed, they were often very aged and did not bear relevance to the processes that Post
Office had improved.

3. As such, Post Office has set about developing a set of postmaster policies across key areas
of postmaster support.

4. Post Office has identified that a comprehensive suite of postmaster policies is required to
demonstrate and ensure GLO compliant support to postmasters in the following areas:

Network Monitoring and Audit Support

Network Cash and Stock Management and Transaction Corrections

Postmaster Account Support

Postmaster Accounting Dispute Resolution

Postmaster Contractual Performance

Postmaster Suspension

Postmaster Termination

Postmaster Appeals

Postmaster Accountability

Postmaster Complaint Handling

5. The policies relating to Contractual Performance, Postmaster Suspension and Termination
are already approved and in use.

6. This paper seeks approval of the policies relating to Network Monitoring and Audit Support,
Postmaster Account Support, and Postmaster Accounting Dispute Resolution. These policies
can be found in the appendices to this paper.

7. The Network Monitoring and Audit Support policy sets out Post Office’s network monitoring
principles which aim to support postmasters with accounting practices, prevent postmasters
from suffering discrepancies and protect both postmasters and Post Office from possible
losses.

8. The Postmaster Account Support policy sets out how Post Office will notify postmasters pro-
actively when an accounting discrepancy has occurred and how it will offer support to help
postmasters understand the reason(s) for any discrepancy. The policy also sets out how
Post Office could deal with the recovery of losses if the loss is established (following
investigation and/or acceptance by the postmaster).

9. The Postmaster Accounting Dispute Resolution policy sets out how Post Office will
investigate and resolve any accounting disputes raised by postmasters.

Tab 12 Mails Fraud Update

@

POST OFFICE LIMITED
RISK & COMPLIANCE COMMITTEE REPORT

Title: Mails Fraud Update Meeting Date: I 12 January 2021
. Tim Perkins, Service and Support . Amanda Jones, Retail and
authors Optimisation Director Spenser: Franchise Network Director

Input Sought: Noting

The Risk & Compliance Committee (RCC) is asked to note a mails fraud identified on 25"
November 2020 through a whistleblow received by an Area Manager from a member of staff in
an agency Post Office. The reported fraud involves branches processing mail items prior to the
branch opening to the public, printing the mail labels and affixing them to the mail items and
then switching off Horizon before the transactions are committed. The whistleblow related to
this fraud being committed at three branches run by one Postmaster.

Executive Summary

Following a whistleblow from an assistant in an agency branch, Post Office has identified a
method of completing a mails fraud where postage labels are printed in branch and used for
delivery of postage items, but no payment for the service is made and there is no onward
settlement with Royal Mail for the items.

Post Office has informed Royal Mail of the fraud and Royal Mail have indicated their intention
to complete an investigation to gather direct evidence of the fraud, so that they can pursue the
parties perpetrating the fraud. At the point of writing this paper, the dates for Royal Mail
completing their investigation are not confirmed.

Post Office will implement a technical fix to prevent the fraud from being able to be completed
once the Royal Mail investigation is completed.

Post Office have further investigated the potential for this type of fraud to be used on other
products, but have not found any evidence that there are similar opportunities in other
products.

However, given a number of significant frauds impacting Post Office in the last 24 months, a
review into Post Office's fraud prevention capabilities will take place in Q4 2020-21. This review
will make recommendations for fraud prevention enhancements to be made in 2021-22.

Questions addressed

How was the issue raised?

How is the fraud performed?

What steps have been taken to deal with the fraud?
What steps are being taken to prevent further fraud?

eport

1. On 25" November 2020, an assistant in an agency branch approached their Area Manager
to report a suspected fraud being undertaken in their branch and in the other branches
run by the Postmaster of the branch.

SWNE

ys)

Strictly Confidential

148 of 183 Post Office Limited - Risk and Compliance Committee-12/01/21

POL-BSFF-0238512_0147
POL00423694
POL00423694

Tab 12 Mails Fraud Update

@

2. The assistant reported that two men entered the branch each morning and completed
mails transactions on Horizon for mails items believed to have been collected from local
businesses. The postage labels are printed in the standard way and affixed to the mails
items before the men power down Horizon, leading to the items in the ‘stack’ being lost
and unrecovered when Horizon is powered up again. The assistant reported that between
20 and 30 mails sacks were processed in this manner per day.

3. The Area Manager raised the assistant’s concerns with the contracts and security teams
for investigation.

4. A preliminary desktop investigation confirmed that the data held by Horizon tallied with
the assistant’s report of the method used to commit the fraud.

5. The preliminary investigation also confirmed that whilst the postage labels were produced,
there is no corresponding sales record in Horizon for these postage labels. Given there is
no sales record for the items, there is no subsequent settlement with Royal Mail for the
service they still need to provide on the items.

6. This means that Royal Mail is the party that is most financially disadvantaged by the fraud.
Post Office and the Postmaster will lose some commission from not settling the
transactions with Royal Mail (assuming the transactions would otherwise be processed
properly through the Post Office). However, if the Postmaster is taking some (non Horizon
settled) payment for processing the mail items, the Postmaster will be gaining whatever
payment they receive for processing the items.

7. Given the impact on Royal Mail, Post Office Security reported the issue to Royal Mail Group
Security on 2" December 2020, once the preliminary investigation had confirmed the
method used to commit the fraud. Royal Mail Group Security indicated that they would be
interested in pursuing the case and putting in place an operation that will gather direct
evidence of the fraud being committed. In order to run such an operation, Royal Mail
Group Security indicated that they would need authorisation and that any such operation
would not take place until January 2021. Royal Mail indicated to Post Office Security that
their favoured approach to investigations like this is to complete a full investigation and
look to seek civil recovery from the fraudulent party. They reported recent success with
this approach in relation to an eBay seller in the same geographical area as the branches
highlighted to them.

8. At the point of writing this paper, Royal Mail have yet to confirm the date they intend to
complete their operation to gather direct evidence.

9. Post Office decided not to immediately suspend the Postmaster of the three branches prior
to the Royal Mail investigation being completed for the following reasons:
a. Suspension of the Postmaster would likely impact the effectiveness of any Royal
Mail investigation (as the fraudulent behaviour may cease),
b. Suspension of the Postmaster may leave Royal Mail with no identified party to
pursue for civil recovery (and recovery may fall on Post Office),
c. Suspension of the Postmaster would be better done once direct evidence has
been gathered through the Royal Mail investigation, and

Strictly Confidential

Post Office Limited - Risk and Compliance Committee-12/01/2 149 of 183

POL-BSFF-0238512_0148
POL00423694
POL00423694

Tab 12 Mails Fraud Update

@

d. Suspension of the Postmaster may impact the whistleblower.
10. Once the Royal Mail investigation is complete, Post Office will consider the evidence and
make a decision on suspending the Postmaster at that point.

11. In addition to investigating the fraud being committed at the branches identified by the
whistleblower, a review has been undertaken across the network to understand the
prevalence of the fraud across the estate over the last 11 months.

12. This review found that 80.25% of the occurrences of labels being printed with no
corresponding sales entry happened in 6 branches. 51.23% of all occurrences were in the
3 branches operated by the Postmaster at the whistleblower’s branch.

13. The potential quantum of the potential fraud at these 6 branches amounts to
£1,026,635.30 using the first class small parcel postage rate of £3.70.

14. In considering the method used in committing this fraud, Post Office have also looked at
which other product types are ‘non-recoverable’ in the event of an unexpected Horizon
shut down.

15. There are a number of other ‘non-recoverable’ products including stock items (such as
stamps) and bill payment items. These other products are unlikely to be used to commit
similar frauds because there is a consequential impact in committing the fraud:

a. For stock items, physical stock would be short against Horizon generated stock
and the branch wouldn’t balance, and

b. For bill payment items, the bill would remain unpaid and would generate a
customer or a client query.

16. In order to prevent future occurrences of this type of fraud, Post Office is working with its
suppliers to implement a technical fix to avoid the loss of the basket data if Horizon
connectivity is disrupted. Post Office expects to be ready to implement this fix once the
Royal Mail investigation is completed. If there is a gap between the completion of the
Royal Mail investigation and the proposed implementation of the technical fix, Post Office
will ensure robust monitoring is in place to identify any further frauds.

17. This fraud follows a number of other frauds that have had significant impact on Post Office
in the last 24 months:

a. A prepaid labels fraud where an operator inflated their commission by c£230k
from Royal Mail by processing ghost prepaid labels,

b. A Parcelforce reversals fraud where c£140k was identified as having been
removed by a Postmaster’s assistant from processing Parcelforce reversals
where no corresponding sales were present, and

c. A banking deposit fraud where a Postmaster was duped into making banking
deposits of c£800k without the cash being introduced at the branch.

18. Whilst Post Office has responded to these frauds through increasing monitoring or
implementing a technical fix (as is planned with the mails fraud), this approach is always
reactive rather than proactive.

Tab 12 Mails Fraud Update

@

19. In order to move to a proactive position, Post Office will engage an external specialist to
make an assessment of its fraud prevention capability and to run fraud penetration testing
on products offered over Post Office counters.

20. This review will be completed in Q4 2020-21 and will recommend fraud prevention
enhancements to be delivered in 2021-22. The initial review will be brought back to the
RCC for oversight.

Stakeholder Implications

21. The main impacted stakeholder from the issue identified by this whistleblow is Royal Mail.
The impact on Royal Mail is financial.

22. In order to mitigate the impact on Royal Mail and in order to approach resolution of the
issue in a collaborative way, Post Office made Royal Mail aware of the issue immediately
once it had confirmed the method of the fraud.

Next Steps & Timelines

23. There are a number of key actions as a result of the identification of this fraud:

a. The completion of the Royal Mail-led investigation to gather direct evidence of the
fraud. At the point of writing this paper, the date for completion of the Royal Mail
investigation is unconfirmed.

b. The Post Office response to the outcomes of the Royal Mail-led investigation. This
will follow the completion of the investigation.

c. The technical fix to avoid the loss of basket data for non-recoverable items if Horizon
connectivity is disrupted. This will follow the completion of the investigation.

d. The review of Post Office's fraud prevention capability and recommendations for
fraud prevention enhancements. The review and recommendations are due to be
completed in Q4 2020-21.

Report
Fraud risks

The following key fraud risks and mitigations have been identified associated with different
phases of HSS (see also Appendix 2 for further detail).

1. Letters to current/former Postmasters:
4 risks (A1-A4 in Appendix 1) associated with communicating about the existence of HSS
and inviting applications from the wrong persons/deceased persons
a. Risks mitigated by use of internal HR addresses and external 3" party address
validation specialists
b. Some queries received from former Postmasters who had moved multiple times
and still received an invitation to join HSS indicate the correct people
2. Claim amounts exaggerated/inflated/inappropriate
2 risks (D1, E1 in Appendix 1) associated with using information to ‘game’ or claim
unreasonable amounts for heads of loss
a. Risks mitigated by confirming quantum of claims is aligned to the quantum
distribution (initially derived from a comparison of the quantum distribution for
GLO claims, then further developed based on typical HSS claims) - exceptions are
subject to additional scrutiny and review
b. Additional risk mitigations to be implemented will involve processing claims in
similar cohorts to limit the risk of sharing of information to game the decisions, or
negotiate after an offer is made
3. Collaboration fraud
a. 4 risks (F1, H1-H3) associated with potential claimant collaboration at Escalation
or Mediation stage of scheme are to be partially mitigated by use of confidentiality
clauses and by handling similar cohorts at similar times to limit information
collaboration
b. Additional mitigation is focused on agreeing negotiating limits with UKGI/BEIS in
advance and limiting negotiation as appropriate
4. Payment fraud
a. 3 risks (G1, G3-G4) associated with making payments to incorrect accounts are
being mitigated by using standard checks when making payments, supplemented
with additional checks to validate all bank accounts prior to making payment
b. Additional checks are being made prior to making offer/payment to ensure payees
are not on sanctions lists etc. which would mean payments could not be made

Eligibility controls

The following key checks are in place to confirm eligibility (see also Appendix 2 for further
detail).

5. Claims from claimants who are not entitled to make a claim
a. Bankrupt claimants - Risk partially mitigated via: asking claimant to confirm
bankruptcy status in initial claim questionnaire; additionally partially mitigated by
checking bankruptcy status with Official Receiver prior to starting to process a
claim; and additionally partially mitigated prior to making an offer.

b. Company claimants - Risk mitigated by requesting full evidence of authority to

c. On behalf of deceased or incapacitated claimant —- Appropriate documentation to
be obtained to confirm authority to act

6. Claims from claimants outside of Scheme eligibility period

a. Dates to which claims relate are confirmed as relevant to the use of HNG-X and
directed to BAU processes if appropriate (e.g. claim relates to current version of
HNG).

7. In addition to the above standard risk mitigations, a random sample (no. to be agreed
with Internal Audit) of claims will be subject to a monthly ‘deep dive’ to investigate if all
process steps have been followed and all relevant evidence is available to substantiate the

claim as required.
8. The results of the ‘deep dives’ to be reviewed at HM Committee and summarised to Board

in monthly reports

Additional review of fraud risks and eligibility controls

9. As a follow-up to Internal Audit review, an additional review of scheme fraud risk
mitigations and eligibility controls will be carried out to determine and address any gaps.

What updates were included and why?

A summary that identifies the changes and updates to the policies and statements have been
added below:

Cyber and Information Security Policy:

The updates mainly consist of some rewording and updating of links/references where teams
have changed but nothing has substantively changed within the policy.

Policy Compliance View

Control standards can be evidenced as working through the annual Internal Audit and Deloitte
maturity assessment and COBIT controls assessment. These assessments provide a
comprehensive review of the policy and controls stated within them to ensure compliance with
the policy is being maintained at all times.

Investigations Policy:

The Investigation Policy has not been updated or been in use for some time. Whilst most
internal investigations are conducted under local, subject-specific policies - such as
disciplinary, or Dignity at Work - it is felt that there should be an overarching policy dealing
with subjects that are not dealt with under such existing local policies.

Therefore this is a master Investigations Policy that brings back the principles of investigations
for all other Group Policies that relate to it. Going forward there is going to be as part of the
implementation process an Investigations Group being pulled together, which will consist of
both the relevant Policy Owners where it is intended that they will review the underlying
processes of the policy and the appropriate MI to ensure all Minimum Control Standards stated
within the policy are being met consistently.

The former policy has been updated to:
a. Include detail on the processes and on responsibilities.

b. Provide that, where there are existing investigation procedures within other policies, they
should be followed.

c. Define roles and responsibilities more, and specifically to have someone appointed with
overall responsibility for the investigation, i.e., the Commissioning Manager, who is responsible
for establishing terms of reference and identifying the policy, legal and regulatory requirements
of the investigation, including any requirement for an investigation to be carried out under legal
privilege.

14.1

d. Equally there would then be an investigator responsible for the actual investigation.
Effectively they have delegated authority through the Commissioning Manager.

Policy Compliance View

As this policy has not been in use for some time the attestation on the policy performance will
need to be made and carried out in the next annual review - some of the control standards are
in place but some will need to be established soon and are currently under review.

Vetting Policy:

The Post Office Ltd Vetting Policy was last updated in 2017 and following an internal audit which
took place in April 2020, it was identified that the policy did not reflect the current organisation
nor how vetting is currently delivered. In addition to this there was limited reference as to how
the various groups should deliver vetting or the use of the vetting matrix to define minimum
vetting levels.

The Vetting Policy as written in 2017 covered Post Office employees as well Postmasters and
their assistants. However, these parties are vetted by two very distinct processes delivered by
different teams. The policy has now been split out into two separate policies (one for employee
and one for Postmaster/Assistants) and both now reflect the approach to vetting, who
undertakes it and outlines the minimum vetting levels.

This is an entire rewrite of the policy.

Policy Compliance View

Given that this policy has been out of scope for some time the attestation on the policy
performance will need to be made and carried out next year.

There are gaps to be addressed but these will need to be dealt with separately and is something
the incoming Postmaster Effectiveness Director and Postmaster Onboarding Senior Manager
will need to review and address. The fact that under the new structure Postmaster Onboarding
and Assistant Vetting will sit in the same space (both working to the Postmaster Onboarding
Senior Manager) should make these gaps easier to address.

Postmaster Policies:

To be discussed and approved in a separate paper present to the Committee by Tim
Perkins.

Approval is being sought for three new postmaster policies to reflect how Post Office will provide
support to postmasters in relation to their branch accounting.

The policies are:

« The Network Monitoring and Audit Support policy sets out Post Office’s network 14.1
monitoring principles which aim to support postmasters with accounting practices,
prevent postmasters from suffering discrepancies and protect both postmasters and Post
Office from possible losses.

e« The Postmaster Account Support policy sets out how Post Office will notify
postmasters pro-actively when an accounting discrepancy has occurred and how it will

the language that is being applied to all policies throughout the group.

one is outdated. The new template will include guidance notes for policy owners to help assist
them in writing their policies. This is to ensure we have a consistent approach to policies been
written and aligned in the same format throughout the Group.

Conclusion

We continue to work with Policy Owners and Company Secretariat to ensure we maintain our

expected. This is a key part of the wider Post Office controls work.

POST OFFICE LIMITED
RISK, & COMPLIANCE COMMITTEE REPORT
Title: Subsidiary Policy Mandate Meeting Date: I 12 January 2021
. Jonathan Hill, Compliance
. I Reena Chohan, People & Policy . A ,

Author: Compliance Manager Sponsor: Director & Ben Foat, Group

General Counsel

Input Sought: Decision

As part of our developing policy framework, the Committee is asked to review and approve the
Subsidiary Policy Mandate, being:

i. Subsidiaries must adopt all Group Policies unless it cannot do so for a legal, regulatory
or other material reason.

ii. Where subsidiaries cannot adopt a Group Policy for one of the above reasons,
subsidiary shall adopt its own policy in that area that is aligned to the Group Policy.

iii. This adapted subsidiary policy must be reviewed and approved by the Post Office
Limited (POL) Compliance team and the POL Policy Owner, who shall confirm
alignment to the Group Policy.

iv. This process would not apply to Post Office Insurance (POI) regulatory policies, being
Risk, Conduct Risk, Vulnerable Customers, Ex Gratia, Remuneration and any other
policies as required by the Financial Conduct Authority (FCA). Where these policies
are adopted by the POI Board, the Company Secretariat shall notify POL Compliance
of the adoption such that their records can be updated;

for onward submission to the Audit, Risk & Compliance Committee (ARC).

Executive Summary

This paper provides a summary of the proposed actions for the Committee to consider with
regards to the adoption of POL Group Policies by POL’s subsidiaries: Payzone Bill Payments
Limited (PZBPL) and POI.

PZBPL are currently undertaking a review of their policies and identifying gaps against the Key
Policy Framework. Where there are gaps, they are either adopting the Group policy or drafting
a new policy to align with the Group Policy but removing elements that are not applicable to
PZBPL. In both instances, the policies are presented to the PZBPL Board for adoption (Group
Policies) or approval (PZBPL policies adapted from Group Policies).

The PZBPL Board questioned whether the amended policies aligned with Group Policies given
that PZBPL is ultimately overseen by the POL Audit, Risk & Compliance Committee (ARC) and
what assurance could be given that this is the case.

POI have also been engaged in this work to ensure the approach can be aligned across the
Group insofar as possible given POI’s regulated status.

Accordingly, a Subsidiary Policy Mandate is proposed to resolve this query as well as give

assurance to the ARC that POL’s subsidiaries is adhering to Group Policies insofar as possible.

Questions addressed in this paper
aie What is the Policy approach for our subsidiaries?

2. How can we get alignment and consistency for Payzone?
3. What are the next steps?

The Report
What is the Policy approach for our subsidiaries?

1. There are currently 28 Group Policies that relate to Post Office’s key risks. Each of these
Policies have been established to set the minimum operating standards relating to their
business area.

2s All of POL’s subsidiaries should be required to adopt the Group Policies on a “comply or
explain” basis i.e. they must adopt and comply, unless there is a specific regulatory,
material or legal reason as to why they cannot.

How can we get alignment and consistency for our subsidiaries?

3. It is proposed that in cases where a subsidiary is unable to fully adopt Post Office Group
Policies due to a specific regulatory, material or legal reason, the subsidiary will adopt
their own policy in that specific area based on the Group Policy and there would be an
approval process in place to approve and capture the variances/amendments being made
to the policy in question. (refer to appendix 1)

4. This would apply to PZBPL in all instances. For POI, wherever possible the relevant
Group Policy shall be adopted on a “comply or explain” basis. However, for policies that
fall under the scope of POI’s status as a FCA regulated firm, it is not appropriate for POL
to have oversight such policies ("POI regulatory policies”). These policies presently
include: Risk, Conduct Risk, Vulnerable Customers, Ex Gratia and Remuneration, but
would also include any future policies as required by the FCA. Oversight for such policies
must sit with the POI Board and would therefore not be subject to the approval process.
Only POI policies that are not associated with POI’s regulated status would be subject to
the proposed approval process (for example, the POI Procurement Policy).

What are the next steps?

5. When the relevant Group Policy undergoes its annual review, the POL Policy Owner shall
be responsible for engaging with subsidiaries on the policy review to either;

i. adapt the Group Policy such that the subsidiary can adhere to it and therefore
adopt the Group Policy; or
ii. ensure the adapted subsidiary version is reviewed and continues to align to the
Group Policy as amended.
6. This will ensure that, where possible, Group Policies are fit for purpose for the Group and
not just POL. This would not apply to the POI regulatory policies.
